ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e8101	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! If we decide today on this present law, the restructuring of the security authorities, then we will adopt an administrative reform that can really be seen: the largest government reform in the Second Republic and an important addition to the previous Wachbody reform, which began in 2002 with the re-establishment of the Federal Criminal Office, the reorientation of the Cobra in Austria, the introduction of the Security Academy, the equality of training from Lake Constance to Lake Neusiedler, the merger of the guards of police, Gendarmerie and Criminal Police and the integration of customs guards. So many reforms have already taken place, and this further step today in the merger of guards and authorities is, as has been said, the important point on the i, which is placed here. This shows that administrative reform is not mentioned in the Ministry of the Interior in itself, but administrative reform is implemented in the Ministry of the Interior. That is why we also have a very modern, efficient police and security structure in Austria. For this reason, we are also a world leader in security. This is a real merit for those ministers of the Interior who have held this office in Austria since 2000. Mrs. Federal Minister Mikl- Leitner, I congratulate you on this step today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] What is actually changing? – It has already been mentioned: Nine national structures will be created from 31 security structures. So the state police officers, federal police directors and security directors are brought together to form a state police department. Nothing changes for the citizen outside. There are now only additional tight guided tours in the security area. Double-tracks are being reduced and, as has been said, new structures are being created that guarantee efficient work. The objective of an administrative reform is to make savings. We can expect these measures, which are being put in place today, to save between €8 million and €10 million, which, of course, remains in the security field, so that we can better combat crime in all its forms, but that we can also take those measures in the future to improve legal certainty in Austria with the creation of national administrative courts and the creation of a Federal Office for Asylum and Foreign Law. I believe that these are very important measures that need to be implemented in the sense of security as a result. I would like to thank all those who have contributed to this reform, to all those who have come to understand the need to implement these reforms. Finally, 400 managers are affected by their staff. So thank you very much to everyone. I think it is a very important and correct step in the right direction in the field of safety.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e8648	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament, ladies and gentlemen! Dear. Ladies and gentlemen at the audience gallery! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! For a year now, I've been allowed to assume responsibility as Minister of the Interior. This is a very exciting and challenging task, and just last year we have set a great deal of course, also set the course for the biggest government reform in the Second Republic. Otto Pendl rightly referred to it as the century project and as a whole, very large milestone. [[President Neugebauer shall take over the chairmanship.]] What is the objective of this government reform? – The aim of this government reform is to bring together nine national police commanders, eight security directors and 14 Federal Police Directorates to nine national police departments, i.e. from 31 authorities, we make 9 authorities. It is particularly important to me to stress that this government reform only affects the top-level structure, namely the top management structure, that there is no change in police inspections, district police officers and city police commanders, that the competence of the District Head Teams as the first-level security authority remains unchanged. Fact is – and this has already been mentioned today – that the leadership structure becomes slimmer, that the leadership structure becomes flatter, that we manage to eliminate double-tracks, triple-tracks, and that we can save € 8 to € 10 million annually in the medium term. In other words, this reform will make us faster, slimmer, more powerful and more efficient, with the aim of reducing the number of staff in the administration in the future in order to have more staff directly on the streets in our cities and in our communities. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Everyone who knows the Ministry of the Interior knows that the Ministry of the Interior is accustomed to responding to current new challenges. Best evidence of this is all that has happened in recent years – and Günter Kößl as a security spokesman has addressed it –: There was a reform directly in the central office. It was the foundation of the Security Academy, with which we have become much more professional in education and training. There was a new set-up of our Cobra. It was founded by the Federal Criminal Office and the Federal Office for Constitutional Protection and Terrorism. The customs guard was introduced. The implementation of the Team 04 project, i.e. the combination of police and gendarmerie, then a big step, was a much debated and criticised step, but today we know that this step was important and correct. The Federal Ministry of the Interior has committed itself to a strategy “INNEN.SICHER”, a strategy that has been developed by many experts, where a very important and central centrepiece is this government reform, which is in the focus today. The implementation of this government reform now, at this time, is important and correct, because many framework conditions around the police are changing. I am thinking, for example, of the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum, which is currently under review, I am thinking of the national administration courts and federal administration courts which are being created entirely new ones with 1. January 2014 is to be given, and I am thinking here above all of the implementation of the budgetary reform, which with 1. ==References====External links== Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It is precisely with this reform that there is also a merger of authorities and guards – a structure that we know works, because we know this from the experience of Vienna, where this model is now rolled out across Austria, where in the future these state police directors are to be headed by a state police director and his two alternates. But, of course, control is important to us, and this control was also discussed yesterday in the Committee on the Interior. This control will not only be internal, but it will also be external – internally through a controlling department, through internal revision, and of course also externally, by the Federal Administrative Courts and Regional Administrative Courts, of course by the Public Prosecutor's Office in the criminal police sector and by the Offices of the State Government in the transport police sector. A principle which has not yet been addressed to me today, but which is very important to me personally, is a principle which we have laid down here: Every task should come into one hand. What does that mean? This means that, for example, the criminal police sector has been in different hands so far, that we had a criminal police department in the Security Directorate, the Federal Police Directorates and the National Police Commanders. In the future, there will only be a criminal police department within the National Police Directorate. That means that it will also be easier for our prosecutors because they have only one contact in the future. In the personnel sector as well: So far, there has been a staff department in the Security Directorate, the Federal Police Directorates and the National Police Command, and in the future there will only be a staff department where the entire area of know-how, knowledge can be bundled, where professionally and factually best work can be done and where there will only be one contact person. How are the future National Police Directorates structured? – Yes, they are very flatly structured, there will only be two levels in the future, i.e. the level of management, the management level, and the level of the department. That means: slim, fast and efficient. I am particularly concerned about contact with the citizens, and it has been mentioned by both Otto Pendl and Günter Kößl, that this reform of course adds value to citizens, that there will be more staff on the streets and that there will be so-called public services, citizens' services in our national police departments and police commissions. Our citizens know these citizens' services from the district headquarters, where, according to the motto of one-stop – thank you: [[Call ÖVP and Greens:One-stop-Shop!]] One-stop shop, offered. That is to say, this is a model of the civil service agency that works for our district headquarters, and these civil service agencies should then also be in the police field directly. What does the schedule look like? – I am pleased that we are creating the two-thirds majority here today in order to lay the legal foundations for the implementation of this reform, thank you very much now! Of course, there is still much to do in the coming weeks and months. We still have the personnel input concepts to complete, we have to complete the space concepts, because if you reduce 31 authorities to nine, of course this also has an impact on space and space. Of course, we still have to work on what future education and training models look like in order to really create the best basic conditions for our employees, so that they can really handle the new task most professionally. At the beginning of summer, we will write out the positions of the National Police Directors and National Police Directors, followed by all other functions. That is, we are on the road well in the implementation process, and you can only be good on the go if you also have alliance partners with whom you discuss this project in advance comprehensively and intensively. That is why, at this point, a very great and warm thanks to our coalition partners, to SPÖ, above all to Mr National Councilman Otto Pendl, where there has been a lot of in-depth discussions in recent months, where we have created a basic structure that is being filled with life, and where we set a common milestone here to come to a modern authority body to present the way to a modern management and finally to implement it. For example, if a bird flies into a turbine or if a lightning hits, it must be reported in advance as an incident. There are also incidents which endanger safety, but where there is no reporting obligation. However, it is important that the pilots report what they see as a safety risk, nevertheless, so that you can get better and better in the field of safety. Now it is true that, once an accident has happened and a pilot has regularly reported many of these safety risks in the sense of safety, there was a risk that the prosecutor's office, when it sees: Aha, who already reported 20 incidents!, automatically assumed that this pilot was particularly careless. That would be an absolute obstacle to prevention! Point Report of the Transport Committee on the Government Template (1730, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Law on Sea Shipping and the Federal Law on the Compliance of the International Ship Survey Convention of 1969 (1746 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e11848	We're now on the 7th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e11877	Mr President. Minister! From aviation to maritime transport: The amendment of the Sea Shipping Act concerns the re-regulation of licences for sailing and motor boats on the sea and the formal withdrawal of Austria from commercial shipping. What does the re-regulation of licences for sailing and motor boats mean on the sea? – So far, state-recognised tests have been a monopoly of the Austrian Sailing Association and the Motorboat Sports Association for Austria. The Constitutional Court annulled this for breach of the principle of equality. In other words, it will now be renewed that other organisations other than the ÖSV and MSVÖ can apply for recognition of the tests at BMVIT. The recognition of an organisation by BMVIT is based on certain quality criteria laid down in § 15 of the Maritime Shipping Act. This has really taken an important step towards abolishing a monopoly. The second is the formal withdrawal of Austria from commercial shipping. Ladies and gentlemen! Since 1918, Austria has lost access to and for itself a high sea shipping company since we had no access to a port. We had our own shipping company in Austria from the late fifties to the 1970s. This was the Ister shipping company with four ships: the "Wienertor", the "Linzertor", the "Kremsertor" and the "Buntentor", which drove until the end of the 1970s. Then these ships were flagged out and the shipping was awarded. There have still been ships flying under Austrian flag. These were three ships, which in 2011 also set sea shipping under the Austrian flag. Now it looks like the EU would force us to make a truly large and elaborate government administration if ships were to continue flying under the Austrian flag. We won't do that to save something. Therefore, Austria will formally withdraw from commercial shipping and remain in inland navigation. I think that this is a right approach, even in the course of the saving idea.

Point Report of the Transport Committee on the Government Template (1636, e.g._Other Organiser Cooperation agreement on satellite navigation between the European Union and its Member States and the Kingdom of Norway (1747, B.C.)
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e12282	We're now on the 8th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. I would draw attention to the fact that only four speakers have spoken. The first is Mrs Mag. Lohfeyer. 3 minutes speaking time is set – please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e12315	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! High house! With the Galileo satellite navigation system, the EU is launching a new innovation cycle with a wide range of potential applications. Galileo is a joint initiative of the EU Commission and the EU space agency ESA and part of the TEN transport project. The first satellites have already been put into space in autumn 2010 and are expected to follow up on a regular basis by 2020. Europe is to have its own modern, independent and global satellite navigation system under civil control, which is geared towards the needs of the economy and which is intended to meet all the users' requirements relevant in agriculture, aviation and maritime transport. The aim is to provide an offer of five different navigation services. The first are planned for 2014/2015. The EU intends to come with this system in providing forward-looking infrastructure at an eye-to-face with other global players, such as the US. The aim of international cooperation is to achieve maximum benefits. Norway has been one of the closest cooperation partners outside the EU since the beginning and definition phase of the Galileo programme, and has played a political and technical role in its membership of the European Space Agency. It already has two important ground stations to contribute to the proper functioning of the system and is also financially involved in the Galileo programme. This Agreement regulates Norway's participation in the programme and aims to strengthen cooperation in various areas such as security, export control, radio spectrum, accreditation and ground facilities. Norway also undertakes to contribute in future Community policies to the protection of this navigation system. BMVIT and the Minister support the development of sustainable technological solutions and thus strengthen the Austrian position in international competition. All five political groups have agreed to this State Treaty in the Transport Committee.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e12356	The next item is the speech by Mr Deputies on jewellery miners. 4 minutes speaking time is set. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e12377	Mr President. Estimated Mrs. Federal Minister! This government template concerns cooperation in the field of satellite navigation between the European Union, its Member States and the Kingdom of Norway. Norway has long been a partner for the Galileo project and a member of European space. The main emphasis here is that Norway has two important ground stations for this Galileo project, from which control of satellites can ultimately be carried out. The ratification of this Agreement also strengthens the EEA Agreement as a whole, and above all cooperation in the field of security, accreditation of radio frequencies and export controls. The European satellite navigation system Galileo is part of the global navigation satellite system. Here, the EU's goal is to make these technologies available to all participants, especially in the future, as we have very strong international developments. We have the GPS system in the USA, the GLONASS system in Russia and the Galileo project in the EU. But China, Japan and India are also very active in the construction and development. Clearly, as the European Union, we must work very strongly in this direction, and there are, of course, partners like Norway always welcome. It is therefore also the objective of developing civil objectives in the Galileo project, but since 2008, the European Union has also been empowered to make available the findings of the Galileo project on European security and defence policy. This is an important step towards security in Europe, but also for economic and civil objectives and for the population. If we have such strong movements internationally, it is very good if we support this cooperation within Europe.

I know exactly in the discussions on investments in infrastructure 50, 60 years ago it was always about whether this would ever be calculated. No one's questioning this today. I have already experienced this even in the case of rail infrastructure investments. Of course, today we can easily argue whether we need a Brenner base tunnel. I believe that in the transit traffic we have in this region, we need a Brenner base tunnel. But this is an investment that we are making today, because we know – that is why we are creating traffic forecasts – that traffic will increase by a quarter in the next few years. If you know this, you can of course put your head in the sand or say that I have to create the conditions for this freight traffic and also the increase of the same is not on the road because of the forecasts, but I have enough capacity on the rail so that it can be transported there. These are, so to speak, long-term investments, and are therefore also refinanced in the long term. This applies not only to the rail and road, but also to the construction of a hydroelectric power plant today. I was connected yesterday at the festival “65 years”, and they have the same refinancing duration, namely 30 years, as we have at the rail. The basic infrastructure – that is what we know about today's railway, which is 150 years old, where we are still driving today, where modernisations have been made on the tracks and on the routes – the main investments – 80 percent are the basic investments – which do not last 30 years, but they hold 100 to 200 years, and the generations will benefit from it. It is therefore also important to tell you this because I want to make a commitment to the development of Austrian rail infrastructure, because I consider the railway and this form of mobility necessary for economic and employment reasons. I think the railway is one of the biggest environmental projects we ever have. We don't need to talk about an electric car when we have an electric fleet like the Austrian Bundesbahnen. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Moser.]] We also need not shy the comparison with others. We are one of the best in the EU's 27 Member States. In punctuality we are European champions. There is indeed a single railway in Europe that has small characteristics in which it cuts off better than the ÖBB. That's one thing, and that's the Swiss train, but only the punctuality, not the examples that came here today. With regard to the question of employees, the Swiss railway has 28 000 employees and 3 000 kilometres of train. The Austrian railway has 40,000 employees and runs 5,000 kilometres of train. In other words, per kilometer, the Swiss Bahn has 9.3 employees and the Austrian eight employees. Even there we do not need to shy the comparison with the Swiss railway. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, as I said: It needs transparency, but it also needs such things as continuity, foresight and foresight when it comes to large investments in infrastructure. This, of course, makes it a little difficult because we are making these decisions today. What you decide today is actually the annual framework plan with two amendments. One is the longer-term financial statement, and we have recorded this with the Ministry of Finance and with the Court of Auditors for good reasons and also for right reasons. The framework plan is available on the website of the BMVIT according to individual federal states, where you can find out each individual measure, each project, the start of construction, the expected start-up, the total cost and which measure will be put in concrete place. The second amendment concerns what we have jointly decided that in all areas we must contribute to the consolidation of our public budget, our budget, that we will do so in the transport infrastructure. That is why we will make € 1 billion in this financial framework in savings and, in order to say it again: The priority is the rail. Even more will we save on expanding the road in Austria, because we all have the responsibility for having a balanced budget, but we also have the responsibility to ensure that mobility in our country is safe and environmentally friendly. And that requires a strong railway.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e14309	Mr President! Federal Minister! High house! The amendments to the University Act, which come to the vote today, have a common manuscript, namely more planning, more clarity and less bureaucracy. I believe that the most important point is the easier recognition of university degrees. I have had the opportunity to be invited as an expert at an international OECD conference, and I can assure myself once again that the competition for the best minds worldwide is long since key forces are in full swing, and that Europe and Austria also have to look at winning the best minds for Austria. With the A-Card we took a very important first step. It is now important to look after key forces within Austria as key forces, to enable them to have a one-stop shop, a central point of contact where recognition comes as quickly as possible, or to decide whether the qualification acquired abroad meets domestic qualification requirements. This is important so that we are competitive, in an educational and scientific union of Europe and Austria. This first step should be accompanied by more information, more service. Statistics show how important this is. Only any third party who is employed in Austria and who has an academic degree in the home country is trying to get it recognized here as well. The second major point concerns the period of inscription: Here we have taken a first step towards more planningability already at the last time. It turned out in the evaluation that here the desire of the universities is to maintain even more planning, because by the double conductivity of being able to register for several studies, there was no such planning. The Federal Minister has immediately complied with this request with a proposal for a law, which has been drawn up jointly with the universities, with the students. Here you have really worked together on improving. It is a nice common final result, which is now all standing, and I therefore hope that we can also decide together on this proposal for a law. There will be a pre-arrangement and re-regulation of inscription, so that there will be earlier security for universities and thus better planning. Ladies and gentlemen, but this planning cannot stop at the time of application. In the joint coalition agreement, we have stated that we want a new model of study space-related funding. A new model of study space-related funding is only possible if we also regulate capacities for universities. That means that we also need approval rules. It is therefore a matter of concern to me, especially as a Member of the 2nd and 20ths. District, where the largest economic university in Europe is built with €500 million, is that this university can offer its future students the conditions that students need, namely also for students planning in the sense of: the lecture hall is really allowed to the number of students who are also studying there. For example, this university, like many other universities, will require access schemes to make the funding of study places really effective. The new building of the largest economic university will revive the Leopoldstadt. It is now important that this university, as well as all other 21 universities, also receives framework conditions with which it can regulate its student numbers and that clear admission schemes apply in the future. We need common instruments to do this now. Mr Federal Minister, good for further negotiations for this!

I expressly say that not only in the case of mineral oil, but also in the case of food and other products. That is the task of the European Commission! We must also call on the Commission to investigate the formation of fuel prices. The EU Commission must adopt appropriate measures. I would like to take this opportunity to refer to a recent study from Germany on the question of why such price increases have recently occurred. In an article on this, it says: “Automobiles in Germany must pay more at the gas station than one study suggests it would be justified by the high oil prices.” Furthermore, here is the following: According to the study, the additional profits in super-fuel have not been primarily incurred at the gas stations, but in the refineries that are mainly in the hands of the five large mineral oil companies.’ What does that mean? . We must take a differentiated approach to the question of how the price is achieved. On the one hand, we have to stop speculation, but then we also have to take a close look at what is actually happening in the refineries. The third point I would like to address is: We need to strengthen the Federal Competition Authority! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Thankfully, there is a competitive package by the government, by the Minister of Justice and the Minister of Justice, of the Federal Minister Mitterlehner. This package of competition provides, inter alia, for the strengthening of the Federal Competition Authority. But imagine: The Federal Competition Authority, however, is not in a position to carry out appropriate investigations into the mineral oil companies because of the current legal situation. At present, the Federal Competition Authority is limited in its investigation activity, in particular in obtaining information from undertakings, because enforcement of the obligation to provide information and to submit documents is only possible by a contract of the cartel court. What does that mean? – We must strengthen the Federal Competition Authority – and I now call on all the parties of the House to do so – so that it can carry out its investigation activities accordingly towards large corporations. [[President Neugebauer shall take over the chairmanship.]] High house! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Today, many correct issues have been addressed and demands raised. I would like to thank the Federal Minister Mitterlehner for his proposal for the corridor regulation, which we need to analyse carefully. I fully agree with Mr Gabi Moser that there are two aspects that need to be discussed: on the one hand, from a social point of view. and, on the other hand, from an ecological point of view. We should come to an agreement together.

This government template, the Federal Law on the Pharmacopoeia, redefines and regulates essential provisions on the production, testing and marketing of medicinal products. This amendment also addresses a question of the EU. With the preparation of the pharmacovigilance book, the European and Austrian versions, public printing is no longer required by law, but this requires a tendering procedure. This creates EU conformity. This amendment also introduces adaptations to the provisions of the Pharmacopoeia and the legislation to existing medicinal products. This medicine book law has been in force since 1980. The transfer of competence for technical studies in connection with the AGES medicinal product has a significant quality and safety of the medicinal products and thus also quality and safety for consumers. The possibility of the Federal Minister for the entrusted of AGES officials with the Presidency shows me that professional competence is very important. Dear ladies and gentlemen! The Committee has already discussed and discussed the request made by Mr Hofer to remove the restriction on the delivery of medicinal plants and alternative medical products. However, I would like to say once again that EU Directive 2004/24/EC does not restrict the supply of medicinal products, but rather facilitates market access or market authorisation for traditional herbal medicinal products. Also homeopathic and other treatment methods are not affected by the guidelines. The access of consumers or patients to such products is also not restricted, because this directive does not provide for it at all. That is why, as in the Committee, we will reject this request. These products are not affected by Directive 2004/24/EC and are available to the public as usual. What is criticised by Mr Hofer's request is simply not a technical one. That is why I can also assume that we will not give this request our approval. European law also provided for approval for traditional herbal medicinal products, including safety tests and clinical trials, by the end of this Directive. The possibility of facilitating the registration of traditional herbal medicinal products has been created at European level by this Directive. The purpose of this Directive was, therefore, to promote and facilitate the placing on the market of traditional herbal medicinal products by means of a simplified registration procedure, and not to prohibit, as you mistakenly think, the placing on the market of traditional herbal medicinal products. This was implemented in the National Medicines Act in 2005. Since 2. The registration of traditional herbal medicinal products in Austria is therefore also possible in January 2006. So far 111 traditional herbal specialities have been registered in Austria. The consumer, Mr Riemer, continues to be free to buy, as necessary, either a registered medicine, a cosmetics or a food. I would like to say, perhaps, because this was also an issue in the Committee: In those cases where treatment requires the use of a registered medicinal product not authorised in Austria, Co-operative, also relevant provisions in the Medicines Act, which provide for the absence of a otherwise legally binding authorisation. In addition, the Introductory Act 2010 regulates the controlled import of registered medicinal products not authorised in Austria, provided that the treatment success with a medicinal product authorised and available in Austria is not expected to be achieved. You see that this directive actually exists. And even if the basic intensification may be very honorable, the request is simply not correct, and therefore we will not give this request our consent. We will, of course, give our consent to the previous request. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e22485	Mr President. Minister! High house! Since 2007, the supply situation has steadily deteriorated with substitute doctors in Austria. For example, the number in Lower Austria fell from 305 in 2007 to 58 in 2011, in Upper Austria from 200 to 78, in the Styria from 70 to 36. In 2010 only two doctors performed substitution therapies in Carinthia. Although in 2011 there are now 16, all further substitution therapies are performed in the ambulance system of Klagenfurt and Villach. In six federal states, there is at least one district where no replacement therapy is active, such as in Germanysberg. In part, the cause of the decline in the number is the strict training regulation of the Minister for Smoking Kallat. To a considerable extent, however, it is also because there is no comprehensive and uniform system of fees for substitution therapy. Although the strict training regulation of smoke-kallat was repealed by Minister Stöger in 2009, this did not mean that the number of substitute doctors could be increased. A uniform fee system is not available until now. The overarching health policy goal in the treatment of drug-dependent people must be to take as many of the estimated 25 000 to 37 000 opioid dependents in Austria into treatment – with the aim of bringing them into a self-responsible life and enabling them to pursue a professional activity. This is a prerequisite for providing comprehensive care with treating doctors. The withdrawal of doctors from substitution therapy deteriorates the supply situation massively as 76 percent of the care is currently covered by established doctors, only 13.8 percent of drug ambulances and 10.2 percent of specialists. The result of the deterioration in the supply situation is an increase in the black market of addictions, increased procurement crime and an increase in follow-up diseases such as HIV. For this reason, fees should be negotiated between the doctor's chambers and local health telephone. But, Mrs Csörgits, the political responsibility of the Minister of Health is to influence the implementation of these negotiations. Mr Minister, speak a word of power in order to ensure the comprehensive implementation of substitution therapy throughout Austria!

ParlaMint-AT_2012-04-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00153_d7e25205	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! As the fourth party, we will agree to this law, a law which would require a much-needed revision, because it dates back to 1890. The regulatory density shows that it was adapted to the requirements of the time, such as the state agreement to appoint an official. We will agree to this law because, as I have already heard in the Committee, there has been this amendment to ensure that all traditions within the religious society are properly represented, because – and that has already been said by my previous speakers – it is not the responsibility of the state to interfere in Jewish conflicts, but we must take the concerns, of course, seriously. After some discussions, we also discussed with representatives of the other parties and also the Cultus community very carefully and decided very carefully what we do with this law or that we give our consent. The main points are that this Israeli religious society is a public law body that can regulate internal affairs independently. It is free in confession and teaching and has the right to free exercise of religion. The Israeli religious society is to be seen as an umbrella association over the individual cultural communities, as a umbrella association with all rights and obligations, with the rights to Jewish holidays, whose protection of the state also has to be guaranteed, with the right to religious education and youth education – that is, teaching content is the internal matter of the religious society, but must not be contrary to the objectives of state education – and also with the right to an annual grant in the form of a fixed amount as set out in the law. I believe that with this joint committee statement, which I have already mentioned, we have been able to calm down the concerns and fears of the liberal Jews, who have appeared in advance, and perhaps also to show that we have really thought here. That does not mean, however, that we as a state should act as arbitrators in internal Jewish conflicts, and not that we should interfere in the autonomy and self-determination of the Israeli religious society and circumcise it. We give our consent because it is a law that sets the cooperation of state and Israel's religious society on modern foundations and complies with modern legal and social conditions.

The present draft is intended to be linked to the basic idea at that time, in doubt for freedom, equal treatment for all legally recognized churches and religious societies, while taking into account their particularities. In addition, today's needs, in particular to construct regulations in such a way that they quickly create legal certainty for all parties involved. I would like to stress that this draft was developed and discussed together with the representatives of the Israeli religious society. This procedure is in line with the long-standing agreement culture in Austria, namely not to make rules for individual religious societies, without according them with those concerned. This seems particularly important to me, not only because it concerns the good Austrian climate of cooperation between state and churches and religious societies, but above all because the history between Austria and the Israeli community was a long, temporarily successful, often constructive and, unfortunately, often painful. This long history is now to be added another chapter by defining the framework conditions for the organization of the Israeli community as freely as possible and at the same time as necessary. It will be the The individual points such as Typus Cultusgemeinde, Seelsorge, holidays, curators' arrangements have already been addressed by Mr Amon. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! State and religions are separated in Austria, and that is good. It's important for both parts. A modern rule of law regulates these conditions by laws whose task it is to create a secure framework for all, the state, the respective religion, its followers and for all other people in our country, a framework in which everyone can move and which is ready for the case of conflicts of solution methodology, as far as this is legally possible. It is also important that the relationship between state and religion is based on mutual respect, recognition of the tasks, needs, desires and concerns of each other. Only in such an environment can a good climate for religions and between religions arise and remain. The religious climate, especially the interconfessional climate in Austria, is seen as very well and recognized. Especially the relationship with the Israelites, to Judaism, was not always marked by these attributes. Here, in the state, society and between the denominations in recent years and decades, a lot has changed and improved. A positive climate does not arise by itself, but by the sustainable and serious work of many people. All these people are to be warmly thanked for their work, and at the same time it is important to ask them not to let down in their efforts. Thank you.
